+We are asking for a tier-raising mathematical diagnosis of the attached manuscript, not a
+journal-placement recommendation. Please read main.pdf and use the account below as the
+claim and priority boundary that we intend to defend. The objective is to identify one
+named, concrete, reachable new theorem that would materially raise the paper's mathematical
+tier. A hard, specific negative is more useful to us than a soft positive. Every objection
+you raise should be paired with the result, argument, comparison, or revision that would
+resolve it.
+
+Objects and notation
+
+Let F_0=0, F_1=F_2=1, and F_{j+2}=F_{j+1}+F_j, and let
+phi=(1+sqrt(5))/2. Let R(n) be the number of partitions of n into distinct
+Fibonacci numbers F_2,F_3,..., so the two occurrences of the unit Fibonacci number are
+identified; set R(n)=0 for n<0. Put
+
+  I_m=[F_{m+1}-1,F_{m+2}-1) cap Z,       m>=1.
+
+For the finite-window model, let Omega_m={0,1}^m and let X_m be the binary words
+x=x_1...x_m with no adjacent 1s. Define
+
+  N_m(omega)=sum_{j=1}^m omega_j F_{j+1},
+  V_m(x)=sum_{j=1}^m x_j F_{j+1}.
+
+The map V_m is a bijection from X_m to {0,...,F_{m+2}-1}. The fold is
+
+  Fold_m(omega)=V_m^{-1}(N_m(omega) mod F_{m+2}),
+
+and d_m(x)=|Fold_m^{-1}(x)|. Write d_m^(#)(r)=d_m(V_m^{-1}(r)) and
+S_t(m)=sum_{x in X_m}d_m(x)^t for real t.
+
+For a reduced p/q in (0,1), write its unique negative continued fraction as
+
+  p/q=1/(a_1-1/(a_2-...-1/a_r)),        a_i>=2,
+
+and put d(p/q)=sum_i(a_i-1) and c(p/q)=2d(p/q)+1. Let sigma_0>2 be the
+unique solution
+
+  zeta(sigma_0-1)/zeta(sigma_0)=2,
+
+so sigma_0=2.478750785733960..., and define
+
+  mu_C=sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1} c(p/q)q^{-sigma_0},
+
+  v_c={sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1}q^{-sigma_0}log q}/mu_C,
+  t_c=-sigma_0,       alpha_+=(1/2)log phi.
+
+The pressure P is zero for t<=-sigma_0. For t>-sigma_0, P(t)>0 is the
+unique solution
+
+  sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1}
+      q^t exp(-P(t)c(p/q))=1.
+
+Main results in the present manuscript
+
+1. Results for the field's standard Fibonacci partition function R. For every real t,
+
+  Z_m^R(t):=sum_{n in I_m}R(n)^t,
+  lim_{m->infinity} m^{-1}log Z_m^R(t)=P(t).
+
+The function P is zero on (-infinity,-sigma_0], positive, real analytic, and strictly
+convex on (-sigma_0,infinity), with P'_-(t_c)=0 and P'_+(t_c)=v_c>0. For every
+s>sigma_0,
+
+  Z_m^R(-s) -> 2/{2-zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)}-1,
+
+whereas at criticality
+
+  Z_m^R(-sigma_0) ~ 2m/mu_C.
+
+For n in I_m with R(n)>2, let g(n) be the generator of its Weinstein orbit and let
+ell_m^R(n)=L(g(n)); put ell_m^R(n)=0 for R(n)<=2. If Nhat_m has the critical Gibbs law
+proportional to R(n)^{-sigma_0} on I_m, then
+
+  (m^{-1}log R(Nhat_m),m^{-1}ell_m^R(Nhat_m))
+       -> (v_c U,U) in distribution,
+
+where U is uniform on [0,1]. If N_m is uniform on I_m, then
+Y_m^R=m^{-1}log R(N_m) satisfies a full LDP with speed m and good rate
+
+  I(alpha)=sup_{t in R}{t alpha-P(t)+log phi}.
+
+Its effective domain is [0,alpha_+], and
+
+  I(alpha)=log phi-sigma_0 alpha             for 0<=alpha<=v_c.
+
+Thus the proof includes local lower bounds for the nonexposed slopes created by the
+pressure corner, not only the Gartner-Ellis upper bound and exposed-slope lower bounds.
+
+The paper also keeps the generating cost. With B_m^R=m^{-1}ell_m^R(N_m), the vectors
+(B_m^R,Y_m^R) satisfy a full LDP on R^2 with speed m. Their limiting log-mgf is
+
+  Lambda(a,t)=max{0,a+P(t)}-log phi,
+
+and their good rate function is
+
+  J(beta,alpha)=(1-beta)log phi+beta I(alpha/beta)
+
+when 0<beta<=1 and 0<=alpha<=beta alpha_+; J(0,0)=log phi; and J=+infinity
+otherwise. In particular,
+
+  J(beta,alpha)=log phi-sigma_0 alpha
+
+on 0<=beta<=1, 0<=alpha<=beta v_c, so the coexistence interval lifts to an
+explicit affine face of the two-dimensional rate surface.
+
+2. Exact finite-window correspondences. Define
+
+  dtilde_m(n)=[z^n] product_{j=1}^m(1+z^{F_j}),
+  Rdagger(n)=R(n)+R(n-1),
+
+and
+
+  pi_m(n)=F_{m+1}n+F_{m+1}-1 mod F_{m+2}.
+
+For every m>=1, pi_m permutes Z/F_{m+2}Z and, for 0<=n<F_{m+2},
+
+  d_m^(#)(pi_m(n))
+    =dtilde_m(n)
+    =Rdagger(n)-Rdagger(n-F_{m+1}).
+
+Pointwise, for every m>=0 and 0<=r<F_{m+2},
+
+  d_m^(#)(r)=R(F_{m+2}+r)   if 0<=r<=F_{m+1}-2,
+  d_m^(#)(r)=R(r)           if F_{m+1}-1<=r<F_{m+2},
+
+with an empty interval allowed in the initial cases. Hence the complete fiber list is
+exactly the list of R on the two consecutive standard layers I_m and I_{m+1}.
+
+3. Exact largest fibers and all maximizers. Let D_m=max_x d_m(x). Then
+
+  D_0=D_1=1,
+  D_{2k}=F_{k+2}                  for k>=0,
+  D_{2k+1}=2F_{k+1}              for k>=1,
+
+and consequently D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4} for every m>=6, without an auxiliary
+recurrence hypothesis. The manuscript also gives every maximizing residue. To state the
+generic part, put G_j=F_{j+1}. For k>=5 define
+
+  I_{2k+1}=G_{k+1}G_{k-3}+1      if k is even,
+  I_{2k+1}=G_kG_{k-2}+1          if k is odd.
+
+For k>=7 define
+
+  I_{2k}=G_{k+2}G_{k-5}+G_3+1    if k is even,
+  I_{2k}=G_{k+1}G_{k-4}+G_3+1    if k is odd,
+
+  J_{2k}=G_{k+1}G_{k-3}+1        if k is even,
+  J_{2k}=G_kG_{k-2}+1            if k is odd.
+
+For m=2k>=10, the maximizing residues are
+
+  {I_{2k+1}-1, G_{2k}-1-I_{2k+1}}.
+
+For m=2k-1>=13, they are
+
+  {I_{2k}-1,J_{2k}-1,G_{2k-1}-1-I_{2k},G_{2k-1}-1-J_{2k}}.
+
+The remaining sets are
+
+  M_0={0}; M_1={0,1}; M_2={0}; M_3={0,1,3}; M_4={0,3};
+  M_5={3}; M_6={3,8}; M_7={3,8,11,16}; M_8={8,16,24};
+  M_9={16,24,29,37}; M_11={37,63,71,79,105};
+
+the generic even formula covers m=10 and m=12. The maximum therefore has exactly two
+maximizers in generic even windows and four in generic odd windows.
+
+4. Local fiber spectrum and frozen unnormalized limits. Let Psi(k) be Weinstein's number
+of generating orbits at multiplicity k and let
+
+  C_m(k)=|{x in X_m:d_m(x)=k}|.
+
+For every m>=1, C_m(1)=2, at residues F_{m+1}-1 and F_{m+2}-1. For every k>=2,
+
+  C_m(k)<=4Psi(k),
+  C_m(k)=4Psi(k) when m>=2k.
+
+Thus the discrete measures sum_k C_m(k)delta_k converge vaguely to
+
+  2delta_1+4 sum_{k>=2}Psi(k)delta_k.
+
+For s>sigma_0,
+
+  S_{-s}(m) -> Zcal(s):=4/{2-zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)}-2.
+
+More exactly, S_t(m) has this finite limit for t< -sigma_0 and diverges to
+infinity for t>=-sigma_0. As s decreases to sigma_0,
+
+  Zcal(s)=4/{kappa(s-sigma_0)}+O(1),
+
+where
+
+  kappa=-[d/ds zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)]_{s=sigma_0}.
+
+For fixed s>sigma_0, the multiplicity Gibbs laws
+C_m(k)k^{-s}/S_{-s}(m) converge in total variation to
+
+  Q_s(1)=2/Zcal(s),
+  Q_s(k)=4Psi(k)k^{-s}/Zcal(s), k>=2.
+
+If K has law Q_s, then, as s decreases to sigma_0,
+
+  E_s log K=1/(s-sigma_0)+O(1),
+  Var_s(log K)=1/(s-sigma_0)^2+O(1).
+
+At the endpoint itself,
+
+  S_{-sigma_0}(m) ~ 4m/mu_C.
+
+If Xhat_m has the finite-window critical Gibbs law proportional to
+d_m(x)^{-sigma_0}, and ell_m(x) is the cost of the generating orbit for d_m(x)>2
+(zero otherwise), then
+
+  (m^{-1}log d_m(Xhat_m),m^{-1}ell_m(Xhat_m))
+       -> (v_c U,U) in distribution.
+
+5. Orbit growth, pressure recovery, and finite-window large deviations. The generator
+counts obey the Tauberian laws
+
+  sum_{k<=x}Psi(k) ~ x^{sigma_0}/(sigma_0 kappa),
+  sum_{k<=x}Psi(k)k^{-sigma_0} ~ (1/kappa)log x.
+
+The paper derives the exact weighted renewal identities
+
+  Z_j^R(-s)=2 sum_{ell=0}^{j-1}u_ell(s)+u_j(s)-1,
+  S_{-s}(m)=4 sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_j(s)+3u_m(s)+u_{m+1}(s)-2,
+
+where u_0(s)=1, u_j(s)=sum_{ell=1}^j b_ell(s)u_{j-ell}(s), and
+
+  b_ell(s)=sum_{p/q reduced in (0,1), c(p/q)=ell}q^{-s}.
+
+These formulas give the critical constants and the marked coexistence laws. The pressure
+statement above is also recovered in these renewal coordinates. If X_m is uniform on X_m
+and Y_m=m^{-1}log d_m(X_m), then Y_m satisfies the same full LDP and rate I as the
+one-layer R model, with effective domain [0,alpha_+] and the exact local exponential rate
+
+  lim_{epsilon->0} lim_{m->infinity}m^{-1}log
+      Pr{|Y_m-alpha|<epsilon}
+    =-[log phi-sigma_0 alpha]
+
+for every 0<alpha<v_c. The new step in that lower bound is a parity-complete orbit-filling
+injection which embeds source generators into every sufficiently large target layer without
+changing multiplicity.
+
+6. Exact finite-prime-support interface, with no claimed directional asymptotic. Fix
+distinct primes P={p_1,...,p_d}. For a nonzero vector a in N^d put
+q_a=product_i p_i^{a_i} and
+
+  A_a(u)=sum_{1<=h<q_a,(h,q_a)=1}u^{c(h/q_a)},
+  A_P(z,u)=sum_{a!=0}A_a(u)z^a.
+
+If U_j(q_n) counts Weinstein generators with R(g)=q_n and L(g)=j, with
+U_0(1)=1, then exactly
+
+  sum_{n in N^d}sum_{j>=0}U_j(q_n)z^n u^j=1/{1-A_P(z,u)},
+
+and, for q_n>2,
+
+  C_m(q_n)=sum_{j>=0}K_m(j)U_j(q_n),
+
+where K_m(j)=4 for j<=m-1, 3 for j=m, 1 for j=m+1, and 0 for j>=m+2.
+At u=1 the series is the rational function
+
+  1/{2-product_{i=1}^d(1-z_i)/(1-p_i z_i)}.
+
+The paper deliberately does not claim a directional coefficient asymptotic or a quenched
+velocity theorem. Already on the dyadic ray the cost has finite first moment but infinite
+second moment and no positive exponential moment. A sharp active-cutoff asymptotic would
+require a lattice stable or semistable local-renewal theorem uniform under multivariate
+exponent conditioning and compatible with the boundary weights 4,3,1; that ingredient is
+not in the manuscript.
+
+Imported results versus claims that are ours
+
+The following are imported and are not novelty claims.
+
+* Sidorov-Vershik's level-m Fibonacci representation frequency is exactly the same finite
+  subset-sum coefficient used here after their convention F_j^(SV)=F_{j+1}; its normalized
+  mass 2^{-m}f_m(k) approximates the Erdos measure.
+* Hu studied local dimensions of the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution. Lau-Ngai gave its
+  exact L^q-spectrum for all q>0. Feng-Olivier proved that the Erdos measure is weak Gibbs,
+  gave its complete spectrum with a negative linear phase, and proved a first-order phase
+  transition. Feng gave the all-real spectrum and the same positive-root/frozen-branch
+  structure. Under the manuscript's convention the exact dictionary is
+
+      P(t)=t log 2-tau_mu(t)log phi.
+
+  Therefore existence of all-real pressure, the positive implicit branch, the frozen
+  negative phase, nondifferentiability at the critical tilt, and the generic Legendre
+  multifractal formalism are prior results.
+* Kocabova-Masakova-Pelantova's classification of the extrema of R and of all maximizing
+  integers is imported. The paper does not claim a new extremal classification.
+* Weinstein's free generator monoid, multiplier multiplicativity, orbit decomposition,
+  eventual layer stabilization, largest-index formulas, and generator Dirichlet series are
+  imported. The Tauberian theorem used on that series is classical.
+* The Stern-Brocot/Knauf denominator pressure, the arithmetic renewal theorem,
+  Wiener-Ikehara, Gartner-Ellis exposed-slope theory, and smooth-point multivariate
+  coefficient methods are imported tools. Sanna's positive-integer power-sum asymptotics
+  and integer zero-temperature endpoint are prior work. Exact single-value formulas and
+  related results of Chow-Slattery, Chow-Jones, Shallit, Kempton, and others are treated as
+  prior context rather than absorbed into our claims.
+
+What we claim as the surviving contribution is narrower: the pointwise affine fold/subset-
+sum/partition-difference correspondence and two-layer interval identity; exact transfer of
+all maximizing residues; the uniform early-layer bound; the cost-weighted renewal in the
+coordinates needed here; the totient/zeta evaluation of Feng's implicit critical condition;
+the constants 2/mu_C and 4/mu_C; the uniform critical coexistence laws; full uniform-layer
+LDP lower bounds at nonexposed slopes, including the joint cost-reward affine face; the
+parity-free orbit filling; the Tauberian constant for Psi; and the exact prime-support
+coefficient interface without an asymptotic claim.
+
+Priority position, stated against our interest
+
+The current audit accepts that the central thermodynamic framework is not ours. In
+particular, the pressure and freezing narrative originally appeared much more novel than it
+is. Sidorov-Vershik, Hu, Lau-Ngai, Feng-Olivier, and Feng are not merely adjacent references;
+they study the same normalized finite representation frequencies and their L^q-spectrum.
+The current manuscript now says this explicitly and includes the affine pressure dictionary.
+Absence of the exact constants or finite-window wording in those papers is not by itself a
+priority proof for us.
+
+The audit also found three live named nearby problems, none solved by the present paper:
+
+* Weinstein (2016), p. 18: describe the set of F-primitive numbers. Our renewal enumerates
+  generators by multiplier and cost but does not prove the order-sensitive comparison
+  needed to identify the least representative at a fixed multiplier.
+* Weinstein (2016), Conjecture 9.1: a convex-hull description of the points (c,F(c)) in the
+  specified layer sets. Our interval identity and extremal theorem determine the top
+  horizontal support and all its maximizers, but not every supporting slope.
+* Weinstein (2022), Conjecture 3.3: the three counts of Fibonacci representations with a
+  prescribed number of parts modulo 3 differ pairwise by at most one, with one pair equal.
+  Our unmarked coefficient/Fourier formulas do not supply the required uniform
+  roots-of-unity cancellation in the part-count variable.
+
+A former possible target, Chow-Slattery Conjecture 1.4, is not open: Zhou's 2023 work states
+that it solves that conjecture and the first three problems on the cited page. It must not be
+offered as a tier-raising target. The three live problems above may also be structurally too
+far from our machinery; please do not select one merely because it is named.
+
+Machine-verification evidence and its limits
+
+The archived reproducibility suite reports 20 passing pytest tests and checksum-pins 15
+scripts, outputs, and data files. Direct exact enumeration checks every finite window
+m=0,...,28 (the largest coefficient array has 832040 entries) for the interval identity,
+closed forms for D_m, the recurrence D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4}, and the complete maximizer
+classification, with zero counterexamples. The real-tilt battery uses windows
+m in {12,16,20,24,28,32} and t from -8 to 10 in steps of 0.25; it checks convexity and
+Legendre-rate shape, frozen-phase behavior, critical constants, weighted renewal identities,
+one-layer and joint identities, local spectra, and the all-real root/layer normalization.
+The archived counts include 3396 local-spectrum checks; 9011 all-real layer checks plus 7938
+bridge checks; 22 symbolic and 210 real-tilt weighted-renewal checks; 44 one-layer checks;
+22 joint one-layer checks; 225 orbit-capacity and 1815 padding checks; and 590 low-reward
+filler checks, all with zero reported numerical failures. A separate exact-rational and
+interval-enclosure calculation proves the strict numerical separation recorded in the
+speed audit and reports PASS.
+
+These computations are sanity checks and counterexample searches only. They are never a
+substitute for proof, do not establish an asymptotic theorem, do not establish priority, and
+do not cure a missing uniformity argument. The manuscript does not claim that this numerical
+battery or a proof assistant has formally proved its theorem chain.
+
+Your previous verdict and what has changed since it
+
+In the previous round you recommended: "Reject; do not recommend handling this as major
+revision and re-review within TAMS." You said that you found no immediate counterexample to
+the core finite identities and regarded the renewal, critical-constant, and nonexposed-slope
+ideas as broadly coherent. But your negative grounds were decisive:
+
+* The priority account was materially wrong because it omitted the direct Erdos-measure and
+  golden-ratio Bernoulli-convolution line. In particular, Lau-Ngai's positive-q formula and
+  Feng/Feng-Olivier's all-real extension and negative first-order transition predated the
+  manuscript, so all-real pressure and freezing could not stand as our headline novelty.
+* You found two load-bearing proof presentations below a TAMS-verifiable standard: the
+  Stern-Brocot/Knauf normalization in the then Lemma 5.7, including possible shift and factor
+  errors, and the parameter-uniform multivariate smooth-point/tail-truncation argument in the
+  then Theorem 5.12.
+* You also requested a fixed Weinstein convention and endpoint lemma, corrected quantifiers
+  and boundary passages in the scalar and joint LDPs, and narrower wording for an operator
+  obstruction. You regarded these as repairable rigor issues, not evidence that the central
+  conclusions were false.
+* Most unfavorably, you said that even after all technical repairs and honest attribution,
+  the remaining results were specialized exact critical refinements, finite-window transfers,
+  and an LDP completion inside an already developed Bernoulli-convolution pressure system,
+  rather than the broad structural advance or deep single core normally needed for TAMS.
+  You expected rejection even after repair and identified Journal of Number Theory as the
+  appropriate level after substantial rewriting.
+
+The present version has responded to, but cannot simply declare victory over, that verdict.
+It now makes Sidorov-Vershik/Hu/Lau-Ngai/Feng-Olivier/Feng central, prints the exact pressure
+dictionary, and labels all-real pressure and freezing as recovery. It adds a term-by-term
+negative-continued-fraction/Knauf bijection, fixes depth and inverse-temperature conventions,
+and prints the first four denominator layers. It no longer asserts the former directional
+prime-support coefficient asymptotic; it retains only the exact generating function and
+states the heavy-tail obstruction. It also spells out the orbit endpoint conventions,
+repairs the LDP parameter order and relative-boundary passage, and makes the one-layer R
+theorem primary. These changes address the priority and proof-form defects you named, but
+they do not by themselves answer your deeper objection that the mathematical increment is
+too specialized to raise the paper's tier. That deeper objection is exactly what we now ask
+you to interrogate constructively.
+
+Questions, in the required order
+
+1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up a tier. State it
+as a theorem with explicit hypotheses and conclusion, not as a topic, program, or vague
+generalization. The result must add independent mathematical content after the priority
+subtractions above. If there is more than one defensible candidate, rank them, but identify
+one first choice. Explain why that exact statement would change a higher-tier referee's
+assessment rather than merely lengthen the manuscript.
+
+2. For each candidate, is it reachable from the machinery already in this paper? Identify
+the precise existing lemmas, constructions, estimates, or dictionaries that would feed its
+proof: for example, the pointwise interval/affine identities, Weinstein free-word and
+weighted-cost renewal, the critical letter law, the orbit-filling injection, the marked
+renewal measure, the local stabilization bound, or the prime-support rational interface.
+Then identify the genuinely new missing ingredient. If the candidate is not reachable from
+what we have, say so plainly and specify what theorem or technique would be needed instead.
+Do not blur "the notation can express it" into "the machinery can prove it."
+
+3. Rank the candidates by success probability times tier impact. Give success probability
+as a number from 0% to 100%, not an adjective. Also score tier impact from 0 to 10 and give
+the product, so the ranking is auditable. Estimate probability for this team starting from
+the present manuscript, not for an ideal specialist with unlimited time. Distinguish a
+proof-risk estimate from a priority-risk estimate when they differ.
+
+4. Decide which of the four standard tier-raising levers applies here, and state exactly
+how:
+
+   (a) settling a named open problem in the literature;
+   (b) proving something about the field's own standard objects, especially R, the Erdos
+       measure, or the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution, rather than about Fold_m;
+   (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one the paper currently
+       assumes for convenience;
+   (d) proving a sharpness or matching-bound theorem showing that an existing estimate
+       cannot be improved.
+
+For any applicable lever, give the exact target statement and the exact prior theorem,
+conjecture, hypothesis, or unmatched bound to which it responds. If none applies in a
+reachable way, say that. In particular, do not call the exact transfer of a known statement
+to Fold_m an instance of (b), and do not treat a closed conjecture as (a).
+
+5. Assume the best candidate is proved in full and the current rigor/priority repairs are
+accepted. What is then the strongest objection a referee at the higher tier would still
+raise? State the objection in referee language and state exactly what would resolve it. If
+nothing realistically resolvable would answer it, identify the structural ceiling.
+
+Hard constraints on your answer
+
+Do not answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know where
+the paper lands as it stands. We are asking what mathematical result would move it up.
+
+Do not propose merely adding more theorems of the kind already present. In an earlier round
+we mechanically accumulated formally new consequences and moved nothing; six of our seven
+papers were then found to have priority omissions, because statements composed from standard
+equivalences can be formally new while carrying little independent priority. We need one
+result with a recognizable external mathematical target, not another layer of transfer,
+repackaging, finite-size constants, or thermodynamic terminology.
+
+If the honest answer is that no reachable result exists and this paper cannot rise, say so
+plainly and explain what structural feature caps it. A clear, well-argued ceiling is useful
+and will be acted on; vague encouragement is not. A hard, specific negative is more useful
+than a soft positive, and every objection must come with what would resolve it.

+We are asking for a tier-raising mathematical diagnosis of the attached manuscript, not a
+journal-placement recommendation. Please read main.pdf and use the account below as the
+claim and priority boundary that we intend to defend. The objective is to identify one
+named, concrete, reachable new theorem that would materially raise the paper's mathematical
+tier. A hard, specific negative is more useful to us than a soft positive. Every objection
+you raise should be paired with the result, argument, comparison, or revision that would
+resolve it.
+
+Objects and notation
+
+Let F_0=0, F_1=F_2=1, and F_{j+2}=F_{j+1}+F_j, and let
+phi=(1+sqrt(5))/2. Let R(n) be the number of partitions of n into distinct
+Fibonacci numbers F_2,F_3,..., so the two occurrences of the unit Fibonacci number are
+identified; set R(n)=0 for n<0. Put
+
+  I_m=[F_{m+1}-1,F_{m+2}-1) cap Z,       m>=1.
+
+For the finite-window model, let Omega_m={0,1}^m and let X_m be the binary words
+x=x_1...x_m with no adjacent 1s. Define
+
+  N_m(omega)=sum_{j=1}^m omega_j F_{j+1},
+  V_m(x)=sum_{j=1}^m x_j F_{j+1}.
+
+The map V_m is a bijection from X_m to {0,...,F_{m+2}-1}. The fold is
+
+  Fold_m(omega)=V_m^{-1}(N_m(omega) mod F_{m+2}),
+
+and d_m(x)=|Fold_m^{-1}(x)|. Write d_m^(#)(r)=d_m(V_m^{-1}(r)) and
+S_t(m)=sum_{x in X_m}d_m(x)^t for real t.
+
+For a reduced p/q in (0,1), write its unique negative continued fraction as
+
+  p/q=1/(a_1-1/(a_2-...-1/a_r)),        a_i>=2,
+
+and put d(p/q)=sum_i(a_i-1) and c(p/q)=2d(p/q)+1. Let sigma_0>2 be the
+unique solution
+
+  zeta(sigma_0-1)/zeta(sigma_0)=2,
+
+so sigma_0=2.478750785733960..., and define
+
+  mu_C=sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1} c(p/q)q^{-sigma_0},
+
+  v_c={sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1}q^{-sigma_0}log q}/mu_C,
+  t_c=-sigma_0,       alpha_+=(1/2)log phi.
+
+The pressure P is zero for t<=-sigma_0. For t>-sigma_0, P(t)>0 is the
+unique solution
+
+  sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1}
+      q^t exp(-P(t)c(p/q))=1.
+
+Main results in the present manuscript
+
+1. Results for the field's standard Fibonacci partition function R. For every real t,
+
+  Z_m^R(t):=sum_{n in I_m}R(n)^t,
+  lim_{m->infinity} m^{-1}log Z_m^R(t)=P(t).
+
+The function P is zero on (-infinity,-sigma_0], positive, real analytic, and strictly
+convex on (-sigma_0,infinity), with P'_-(t_c)=0 and P'_+(t_c)=v_c>0. For every
+s>sigma_0,
+
+  Z_m^R(-s) -> 2/{2-zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)}-1,
+
+whereas at criticality
+
+  Z_m^R(-sigma_0) ~ 2m/mu_C.
+
+For n in I_m with R(n)>2, let g(n) be the generator of its Weinstein orbit and let
+ell_m^R(n)=L(g(n)); put ell_m^R(n)=0 for R(n)<=2. If Nhat_m has the critical Gibbs law
+proportional to R(n)^{-sigma_0} on I_m, then
+
+  (m^{-1}log R(Nhat_m),m^{-1}ell_m^R(Nhat_m))
+       -> (v_c U,U) in distribution,
+
+where U is uniform on [0,1]. If N_m is uniform on I_m, then
+Y_m^R=m^{-1}log R(N_m) satisfies a full LDP with speed m and good rate
+
+  I(alpha)=sup_{t in R}{t alpha-P(t)+log phi}.
+
+Its effective domain is [0,alpha_+], and
+
+  I(alpha)=log phi-sigma_0 alpha             for 0<=alpha<=v_c.
+
+Thus the proof includes local lower bounds for the nonexposed slopes created by the
+pressure corner, not only the Gartner-Ellis upper bound and exposed-slope lower bounds.
+
+The paper also keeps the generating cost. With B_m^R=m^{-1}ell_m^R(N_m), the vectors
+(B_m^R,Y_m^R) satisfy a full LDP on R^2 with speed m. Their limiting log-mgf is
+
+  Lambda(a,t)=max{0,a+P(t)}-log phi,
+
+and their good rate function is
+
+  J(beta,alpha)=(1-beta)log phi+beta I(alpha/beta)
+
+when 0<beta<=1 and 0<=alpha<=beta alpha_+; J(0,0)=log phi; and J=+infinity
+otherwise. In particular,
+
+  J(beta,alpha)=log phi-sigma_0 alpha
+
+on 0<=beta<=1, 0<=alpha<=beta v_c, so the coexistence interval lifts to an
+explicit affine face of the two-dimensional rate surface.
+
+2. Exact finite-window correspondences. Define
+
+  dtilde_m(n)=[z^n] product_{j=1}^m(1+z^{F_j}),
+  Rdagger(n)=R(n)+R(n-1),
+
+and
+
+  pi_m(n)=F_{m+1}n+F_{m+1}-1 mod F_{m+2}.
+
+For every m>=1, pi_m permutes Z/F_{m+2}Z and, for 0<=n<F_{m+2},
+
+  d_m^(#)(pi_m(n))
+    =dtilde_m(n)
+    =Rdagger(n)-Rdagger(n-F_{m+1}).
+
+Pointwise, for every m>=0 and 0<=r<F_{m+2},
+
+  d_m^(#)(r)=R(F_{m+2}+r)   if 0<=r<=F_{m+1}-2,
+  d_m^(#)(r)=R(r)           if F_{m+1}-1<=r<F_{m+2},
+
+with an empty interval allowed in the initial cases. Hence the complete fiber list is
+exactly the list of R on the two consecutive standard layers I_m and I_{m+1}.
+
+3. Exact largest fibers and all maximizers. Let D_m=max_x d_m(x). Then
+
+  D_0=D_1=1,
+  D_{2k}=F_{k+2}                  for k>=0,
+  D_{2k+1}=2F_{k+1}              for k>=1,
+
+and consequently D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4} for every m>=6, without an auxiliary
+recurrence hypothesis. The manuscript also gives every maximizing residue. To state the
+generic part, put G_j=F_{j+1}. For k>=5 define
+
+  I_{2k+1}=G_{k+1}G_{k-3}+1      if k is even,
+  I_{2k+1}=G_kG_{k-2}+1          if k is odd.
+
+For k>=7 define
+
+  I_{2k}=G_{k+2}G_{k-5}+G_3+1    if k is even,
+  I_{2k}=G_{k+1}G_{k-4}+G_3+1    if k is odd,
+
+  J_{2k}=G_{k+1}G_{k-3}+1        if k is even,
+  J_{2k}=G_kG_{k-2}+1            if k is odd.
+
+For m=2k>=10, the maximizing residues are
+
+  {I_{2k+1}-1, G_{2k}-1-I_{2k+1}}.
+
+For m=2k-1>=13, they are
+
+  {I_{2k}-1,J_{2k}-1,G_{2k-1}-1-I_{2k},G_{2k-1}-1-J_{2k}}.
+
+The remaining sets are
+
+  M_0={0}; M_1={0,1}; M_2={0}; M_3={0,1,3}; M_4={0,3};
+  M_5={3}; M_6={3,8}; M_7={3,8,11,16}; M_8={8,16,24};
+  M_9={16,24,29,37}; M_11={37,63,71,79,105};
+
+the generic even formula covers m=10 and m=12. The maximum therefore has exactly two
+maximizers in generic even windows and four in generic odd windows.
+
+4. Local fiber spectrum and frozen unnormalized limits. Let Psi(k) be Weinstein's number
+of generating orbits at multiplicity k and let
+
+  C_m(k)=|{x in X_m:d_m(x)=k}|.
+
+For every m>=1, C_m(1)=2, at residues F_{m+1}-1 and F_{m+2}-1. For every k>=2,
+
+  C_m(k)<=4Psi(k),
+  C_m(k)=4Psi(k) when m>=2k.
+
+Thus the discrete measures sum_k C_m(k)delta_k converge vaguely to
+
+  2delta_1+4 sum_{k>=2}Psi(k)delta_k.
+
+For s>sigma_0,
+
+  S_{-s}(m) -> Zcal(s):=4/{2-zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)}-2.
+
+More exactly, S_t(m) has this finite limit for t< -sigma_0 and diverges to
+infinity for t>=-sigma_0. As s decreases to sigma_0,
+
+  Zcal(s)=4/{kappa(s-sigma_0)}+O(1),
+
+where
+
+  kappa=-[d/ds zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)]_{s=sigma_0}.
+
+For fixed s>sigma_0, the multiplicity Gibbs laws
+C_m(k)k^{-s}/S_{-s}(m) converge in total variation to
+
+  Q_s(1)=2/Zcal(s),
+  Q_s(k)=4Psi(k)k^{-s}/Zcal(s), k>=2.
+
+If K has law Q_s, then, as s decreases to sigma_0,
+
+  E_s log K=1/(s-sigma_0)+O(1),
+  Var_s(log K)=1/(s-sigma_0)^2+O(1).
+
+At the endpoint itself,
+
+  S_{-sigma_0}(m) ~ 4m/mu_C.
+
+If Xhat_m has the finite-window critical Gibbs law proportional to
+d_m(x)^{-sigma_0}, and ell_m(x) is the cost of the generating orbit for d_m(x)>2
+(zero otherwise), then
+
+  (m^{-1}log d_m(Xhat_m),m^{-1}ell_m(Xhat_m))
+       -> (v_c U,U) in distribution.
+
+5. Orbit growth, pressure recovery, and finite-window large deviations. The generator
+counts obey the Tauberian laws
+
+  sum_{k<=x}Psi(k) ~ x^{sigma_0}/(sigma_0 kappa),
+  sum_{k<=x}Psi(k)k^{-sigma_0} ~ (1/kappa)log x.
+
+The paper derives the exact weighted renewal identities
+
+  Z_j^R(-s)=2 sum_{ell=0}^{j-1}u_ell(s)+u_j(s)-1,
+  S_{-s}(m)=4 sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_j(s)+3u_m(s)+u_{m+1}(s)-2,
+
+where u_0(s)=1, u_j(s)=sum_{ell=1}^j b_ell(s)u_{j-ell}(s), and
+
+  b_ell(s)=sum_{p/q reduced in (0,1), c(p/q)=ell}q^{-s}.
+
+These formulas give the critical constants and the marked coexistence laws. The pressure
+statement above is also recovered in these renewal coordinates. If X_m is uniform on X_m
+and Y_m=m^{-1}log d_m(X_m), then Y_m satisfies the same full LDP and rate I as the
+one-layer R model, with effective domain [0,alpha_+] and the exact local exponential rate
+
+  lim_{epsilon->0} lim_{m->infinity}m^{-1}log
+      Pr{|Y_m-alpha|<epsilon}
+    =-[log phi-sigma_0 alpha]
+
+for every 0<alpha<v_c. The new step in that lower bound is a parity-complete orbit-filling
+injection which embeds source generators into every sufficiently large target layer without
+changing multiplicity.
+
+6. Exact finite-prime-support interface, with no claimed directional asymptotic. Fix
+distinct primes P={p_1,...,p_d}. For a nonzero vector a in N^d put
+q_a=product_i p_i^{a_i} and
+
+  A_a(u)=sum_{1<=h<q_a,(h,q_a)=1}u^{c(h/q_a)},
+  A_P(z,u)=sum_{a!=0}A_a(u)z^a.
+
+If U_j(q_n) counts Weinstein generators with R(g)=q_n and L(g)=j, with
+U_0(1)=1, then exactly
+
+  sum_{n in N^d}sum_{j>=0}U_j(q_n)z^n u^j=1/{1-A_P(z,u)},
+
+and, for q_n>2,
+
+  C_m(q_n)=sum_{j>=0}K_m(j)U_j(q_n),
+
+where K_m(j)=4 for j<=m-1, 3 for j=m, 1 for j=m+1, and 0 for j>=m+2.
+At u=1 the series is the rational function
+
+  1/{2-product_{i=1}^d(1-z_i)/(1-p_i z_i)}.
+
+The paper deliberately does not claim a directional coefficient asymptotic or a quenched
+velocity theorem. Already on the dyadic ray the cost has finite first moment but infinite
+second moment and no positive exponential moment. A sharp active-cutoff asymptotic would
+require a lattice stable or semistable local-renewal theorem uniform under multivariate
+exponent conditioning and compatible with the boundary weights 4,3,1; that ingredient is
+not in the manuscript.
+
+Imported results versus claims that are ours
+
+The following are imported and are not novelty claims.
+
+* Sidorov-Vershik's level-m Fibonacci representation frequency is exactly the same finite
+  subset-sum coefficient used here after their convention F_j^(SV)=F_{j+1}; its normalized
+  mass 2^{-m}f_m(k) approximates the Erdos measure.
+* Hu studied local dimensions of the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution. Lau-Ngai gave its
+  exact L^q-spectrum for all q>0. Feng-Olivier proved that the Erdos measure is weak Gibbs,
+  gave its complete spectrum with a negative linear phase, and proved a first-order phase
+  transition. Feng gave the all-real spectrum and the same positive-root/frozen-branch
+  structure. Under the manuscript's convention the exact dictionary is
+
+      P(t)=t log 2-tau_mu(t)log phi.
+
+  Therefore existence of all-real pressure, the positive implicit branch, the frozen
+  negative phase, nondifferentiability at the critical tilt, and the generic Legendre
+  multifractal formalism are prior results.
+* Kocabova-Masakova-Pelantova's classification of the extrema of R and of all maximizing
+  integers is imported. The paper does not claim a new extremal classification.
+* Weinstein's free generator monoid, multiplier multiplicativity, orbit decomposition,
+  eventual layer stabilization, largest-index formulas, and generator Dirichlet series are
+  imported. The Tauberian theorem used on that series is classical.
+* The Stern-Brocot/Knauf denominator pressure, the arithmetic renewal theorem,
+  Wiener-Ikehara, Gartner-Ellis exposed-slope theory, and smooth-point multivariate
+  coefficient methods are imported tools. Sanna's positive-integer power-sum asymptotics
+  and integer zero-temperature endpoint are prior work. Exact single-value formulas and
+  related results of Chow-Slattery, Chow-Jones, Shallit, Kempton, and others are treated as
+  prior context rather than absorbed into our claims.
+
+What we claim as the surviving contribution is narrower: the pointwise affine fold/subset-
+sum/partition-difference correspondence and two-layer interval identity; exact transfer of
+all maximizing residues; the uniform early-layer bound; the cost-weighted renewal in the
+coordinates needed here; the totient/zeta evaluation of Feng's implicit critical condition;
+the constants 2/mu_C and 4/mu_C; the uniform critical coexistence laws; full uniform-layer
+LDP lower bounds at nonexposed slopes, including the joint cost-reward affine face; the
+parity-free orbit filling; the Tauberian constant for Psi; and the exact prime-support
+coefficient interface without an asymptotic claim.
+
+Priority position, stated against our interest
+
+The current audit accepts that the central thermodynamic framework is not ours. In
+particular, the pressure and freezing narrative originally appeared much more novel than it
+is. Sidorov-Vershik, Hu, Lau-Ngai, Feng-Olivier, and Feng are not merely adjacent references;
+they study the same normalized finite representation frequencies and their L^q-spectrum.
+The current manuscript now says this explicitly and includes the affine pressure dictionary.
+Absence of the exact constants or finite-window wording in those papers is not by itself a
+priority proof for us.
+
+The audit also found three live named nearby problems, none solved by the present paper:
+
+* Weinstein (2016), p. 18: describe the set of F-primitive numbers. Our renewal enumerates
+  generators by multiplier and cost but does not prove the order-sensitive comparison
+  needed to identify the least representative at a fixed multiplier.
+* Weinstein (2016), Conjecture 9.1: a convex-hull description of the points (c,F(c)) in the
+  specified layer sets. Our interval identity and extremal theorem determine the top
+  horizontal support and all its maximizers, but not every supporting slope.
+* Weinstein (2022), Conjecture 3.3: the three counts of Fibonacci representations with a
+  prescribed number of parts modulo 3 differ pairwise by at most one, with one pair equal.
+  Our unmarked coefficient/Fourier formulas do not supply the required uniform
+  roots-of-unity cancellation in the part-count variable.
+
+A former possible target, Chow-Slattery Conjecture 1.4, is not open: Zhou's 2023 work states
+that it solves that conjecture and the first three problems on the cited page. It must not be
+offered as a tier-raising target. The three live problems above may also be structurally too
+far from our machinery; please do not select one merely because it is named.
+
+Machine-verification evidence and its limits
+
+The archived reproducibility suite reports 20 passing pytest tests and checksum-pins 15
+scripts, outputs, and data files. Direct exact enumeration checks every finite window
+m=0,...,28 (the largest coefficient array has 832040 entries) for the interval identity,
+closed forms for D_m, the recurrence D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4}, and the complete maximizer
+classification, with zero counterexamples. The real-tilt battery uses windows
+m in {12,16,20,24,28,32} and t from -8 to 10 in steps of 0.25; it checks convexity and
+Legendre-rate shape, frozen-phase behavior, critical constants, weighted renewal identities,
+one-layer and joint identities, local spectra, and the all-real root/layer normalization.
+The archived counts include 3396 local-spectrum checks; 9011 all-real layer checks plus 7938
+bridge checks; 22 symbolic and 210 real-tilt weighted-renewal checks; 44 one-layer checks;
+22 joint one-layer checks; 225 orbit-capacity and 1815 padding checks; and 590 low-reward
+filler checks, all with zero reported numerical failures. A separate exact-rational and
+interval-enclosure calculation proves the strict numerical separation recorded in the
+speed audit and reports PASS.
+
+These computations are sanity checks and counterexample searches only. They are never a
+substitute for proof, do not establish an asymptotic theorem, do not establish priority, and
+do not cure a missing uniformity argument. The manuscript does not claim that this numerical
+battery or a proof assistant has formally proved its theorem chain.
+
+Your previous verdict and what has changed since it
+
+In the previous round you recommended: "Reject; do not recommend handling this as major
+revision and re-review within TAMS." You said that you found no immediate counterexample to
+the core finite identities and regarded the renewal, critical-constant, and nonexposed-slope
+ideas as broadly coherent. But your negative grounds were decisive:
+
+* The priority account was materially wrong because it omitted the direct Erdos-measure and
+  golden-ratio Bernoulli-convolution line. In particular, Lau-Ngai's positive-q formula and
+  Feng/Feng-Olivier's all-real extension and negative first-order transition predated the
+  manuscript, so all-real pressure and freezing could not stand as our headline novelty.
+* You found two load-bearing proof presentations below a TAMS-verifiable standard: the
+  Stern-Brocot/Knauf normalization in the then Lemma 5.7, including possible shift and factor
+  errors, and the parameter-uniform multivariate smooth-point/tail-truncation argument in the
+  then Theorem 5.12.
+* You also requested a fixed Weinstein convention and endpoint lemma, corrected quantifiers
+  and boundary passages in the scalar and joint LDPs, and narrower wording for an operator
+  obstruction. You regarded these as repairable rigor issues, not evidence that the central
+  conclusions were false.
+* Most unfavorably, you said that even after all technical repairs and honest attribution,
+  the remaining results were specialized exact critical refinements, finite-window transfers,
+  and an LDP completion inside an already developed Bernoulli-convolution pressure system,
+  rather than the broad structural advance or deep single core normally needed for TAMS.
+  You expected rejection even after repair and identified Journal of Number Theory as the
+  appropriate level after substantial rewriting.
+
+The present version has responded to, but cannot simply declare victory over, that verdict.
+It now makes Sidorov-Vershik/Hu/Lau-Ngai/Feng-Olivier/Feng central, prints the exact pressure
+dictionary, and labels all-real pressure and freezing as recovery. It adds a term-by-term
+negative-continued-fraction/Knauf bijection, fixes depth and inverse-temperature conventions,
+and prints the first four denominator layers. It no longer asserts the former directional
+prime-support coefficient asymptotic; it retains only the exact generating function and
+states the heavy-tail obstruction. It also spells out the orbit endpoint conventions,
+repairs the LDP parameter order and relative-boundary passage, and makes the one-layer R
+theorem primary. These changes address the priority and proof-form defects you named, but
+they do not by themselves answer your deeper objection that the mathematical increment is
+too specialized to raise the paper's tier. That deeper objection is exactly what we now ask
+you to interrogate constructively.
+
+Questions, in the required order
+
+1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up a tier. State it
+as a theorem with explicit hypotheses and conclusion, not as a topic, program, or vague
+generalization. The result must add independent mathematical content after the priority
+subtractions above. If there is more than one defensible candidate, rank them, but identify
+one first choice. Explain why that exact statement would change a higher-tier referee's
+assessment rather than merely lengthen the manuscript.
+
+2. For each candidate, is it reachable from the machinery already in this paper? Identify
+the precise existing lemmas, constructions, estimates, or dictionaries that would feed its
+proof: for example, the pointwise interval/affine identities, Weinstein free-word and
+weighted-cost renewal, the critical letter law, the orbit-filling injection, the marked
+renewal measure, the local stabilization bound, or the prime-support rational interface.
+Then identify the genuinely new missing ingredient. If the candidate is not reachable from
+what we have, say so plainly and specify what theorem or technique would be needed instead.
+Do not blur "the notation can express it" into "the machinery can prove it."
+
+3. Rank the candidates by success probability times tier impact. Give success probability
+as a number from 0% to 100%, not an adjective. Also score tier impact from 0 to 10 and give
+the product, so the ranking is auditable. Estimate probability for this team starting from
+the present manuscript, not for an ideal specialist with unlimited time. Distinguish a
+proof-risk estimate from a priority-risk estimate when they differ.
+
+4. Decide which of the four standard tier-raising levers applies here, and state exactly
+how:
+
+   (a) settling a named open problem in the literature;
+   (b) proving something about the field's own standard objects, especially R, the Erdos
+       measure, or the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution, rather than about Fold_m;
+   (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one the paper currently
+       assumes for convenience;
+   (d) proving a sharpness or matching-bound theorem showing that an existing estimate
+       cannot be improved.
+
+For any applicable lever, give the exact target statement and the exact prior theorem,
+conjecture, hypothesis, or unmatched bound to which it responds. If none applies in a
+reachable way, say that. In particular, do not call the exact transfer of a known statement
+to Fold_m an instance of (b), and do not treat a closed conjecture as (a).
+
+5. Assume the best candidate is proved in full and the current rigor/priority repairs are
+accepted. What is then the strongest objection a referee at the higher tier would still
+raise? State the objection in referee language and state exactly what would resolve it. If
+nothing realistically resolvable would answer it, identify the structural ceiling.
+
+Hard constraints on your answer
+
+Do not answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know where
+the paper lands as it stands. We are asking what mathematical result would move it up.
+
+Do not propose merely adding more theorems of the kind already present. In an earlier round
+we mechanically accumulated formally new consequences and moved nothing; six of our seven
+papers were then found to have priority omissions, because statements composed from standard
+equivalences can be formally new while carrying little independent priority. We need one
+result with a recognizable external mathematical target, not another layer of transfer,
+repackaging, finite-size constants, or thermodynamic terminology.
+
+If the honest answer is that no reachable result exists and this paper cannot rise, say so
+plainly and explain what structural feature caps it. A clear, well-argued ceiling is useful
+and will be acted on; vague encouragement is not. A hard, specific negative is more useful
+than a soft positive, and every objection must come with what would resolve it.

+We are asking for a tier-raising mathematical diagnosis of the attached manuscript, not a
+journal-placement recommendation. Please read main.pdf and use the account below as the
+claim and priority boundary that we intend to defend. The objective is to identify one
+named, concrete, reachable new theorem that would materially raise the paper's mathematical
+tier. A hard, specific negative is more useful to us than a soft positive. Every objection
+you raise should be paired with the result, argument, comparison, or revision that would
+resolve it.
+
+Objects and notation
+
+Let F_0=0, F_1=F_2=1, and F_{j+2}=F_{j+1}+F_j, and let
+phi=(1+sqrt(5))/2. Let R(n) be the number of partitions of n into distinct
+Fibonacci numbers F_2,F_3,..., so the two occurrences of the unit Fibonacci number are
+identified; set R(n)=0 for n<0. Put
+
+  I_m=[F_{m+1}-1,F_{m+2}-1) cap Z,       m>=1.
+
+For the finite-window model, let Omega_m={0,1}^m and let X_m be the binary words
+x=x_1...x_m with no adjacent 1s. Define
+
+  N_m(omega)=sum_{j=1}^m omega_j F_{j+1},
+  V_m(x)=sum_{j=1}^m x_j F_{j+1}.
+
+The map V_m is a bijection from X_m to {0,...,F_{m+2}-1}. The fold is
+
+  Fold_m(omega)=V_m^{-1}(N_m(omega) mod F_{m+2}),
+
+and d_m(x)=|Fold_m^{-1}(x)|. Write d_m^(#)(r)=d_m(V_m^{-1}(r)) and
+S_t(m)=sum_{x in X_m}d_m(x)^t for real t.
+
+For a reduced p/q in (0,1), write its unique negative continued fraction as
+
+  p/q=1/(a_1-1/(a_2-...-1/a_r)),        a_i>=2,
+
+and put d(p/q)=sum_i(a_i-1) and c(p/q)=2d(p/q)+1. Let sigma_0>2 be the
+unique solution
+
+  zeta(sigma_0-1)/zeta(sigma_0)=2,
+
+so sigma_0=2.478750785733960..., and define
+
+  mu_C=sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1} c(p/q)q^{-sigma_0},
+
+  v_c={sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1}q^{-sigma_0}log q}/mu_C,
+  t_c=-sigma_0,       alpha_+=(1/2)log phi.
+
+The pressure P is zero for t<=-sigma_0. For t>-sigma_0, P(t)>0 is the
+unique solution
+
+  sum_{q>=2} sum_{1<=p<q,(p,q)=1}
+      q^t exp(-P(t)c(p/q))=1.
+
+Main results in the present manuscript
+
+1. Results for the field's standard Fibonacci partition function R. For every real t,
+
+  Z_m^R(t):=sum_{n in I_m}R(n)^t,
+  lim_{m->infinity} m^{-1}log Z_m^R(t)=P(t).
+
+The function P is zero on (-infinity,-sigma_0], positive, real analytic, and strictly
+convex on (-sigma_0,infinity), with P'_-(t_c)=0 and P'_+(t_c)=v_c>0. For every
+s>sigma_0,
+
+  Z_m^R(-s) -> 2/{2-zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)}-1,
+
+whereas at criticality
+
+  Z_m^R(-sigma_0) ~ 2m/mu_C.
+
+For n in I_m with R(n)>2, let g(n) be the generator of its Weinstein orbit and let
+ell_m^R(n)=L(g(n)); put ell_m^R(n)=0 for R(n)<=2. If Nhat_m has the critical Gibbs law
+proportional to R(n)^{-sigma_0} on I_m, then
+
+  (m^{-1}log R(Nhat_m),m^{-1}ell_m^R(Nhat_m))
+       -> (v_c U,U) in distribution,
+
+where U is uniform on [0,1]. If N_m is uniform on I_m, then
+Y_m^R=m^{-1}log R(N_m) satisfies a full LDP with speed m and good rate
+
+  I(alpha)=sup_{t in R}{t alpha-P(t)+log phi}.
+
+Its effective domain is [0,alpha_+], and
+
+  I(alpha)=log phi-sigma_0 alpha             for 0<=alpha<=v_c.
+
+Thus the proof includes local lower bounds for the nonexposed slopes created by the
+pressure corner, not only the Gartner-Ellis upper bound and exposed-slope lower bounds.
+
+The paper also keeps the generating cost. With B_m^R=m^{-1}ell_m^R(N_m), the vectors
+(B_m^R,Y_m^R) satisfy a full LDP on R^2 with speed m. Their limiting log-mgf is
+
+  Lambda(a,t)=max{0,a+P(t)}-log phi,
+
+and their good rate function is
+
+  J(beta,alpha)=(1-beta)log phi+beta I(alpha/beta)
+
+when 0<beta<=1 and 0<=alpha<=beta alpha_+; J(0,0)=log phi; and J=+infinity
+otherwise. In particular,
+
+  J(beta,alpha)=log phi-sigma_0 alpha
+
+on 0<=beta<=1, 0<=alpha<=beta v_c, so the coexistence interval lifts to an
+explicit affine face of the two-dimensional rate surface.
+
+2. Exact finite-window correspondences. Define
+
+  dtilde_m(n)=[z^n] product_{j=1}^m(1+z^{F_j}),
+  Rdagger(n)=R(n)+R(n-1),
+
+and
+
+  pi_m(n)=F_{m+1}n+F_{m+1}-1 mod F_{m+2}.
+
+For every m>=1, pi_m permutes Z/F_{m+2}Z and, for 0<=n<F_{m+2},
+
+  d_m^(#)(pi_m(n))
+    =dtilde_m(n)
+    =Rdagger(n)-Rdagger(n-F_{m+1}).
+
+Pointwise, for every m>=0 and 0<=r<F_{m+2},
+
+  d_m^(#)(r)=R(F_{m+2}+r)   if 0<=r<=F_{m+1}-2,
+  d_m^(#)(r)=R(r)           if F_{m+1}-1<=r<F_{m+2},
+
+with an empty interval allowed in the initial cases. Hence the complete fiber list is
+exactly the list of R on the two consecutive standard layers I_m and I_{m+1}.
+
+3. Exact largest fibers and all maximizers. Let D_m=max_x d_m(x). Then
+
+  D_0=D_1=1,
+  D_{2k}=F_{k+2}                  for k>=0,
+  D_{2k+1}=2F_{k+1}              for k>=1,
+
+and consequently D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4} for every m>=6, without an auxiliary
+recurrence hypothesis. The manuscript also gives every maximizing residue. To state the
+generic part, put G_j=F_{j+1}. For k>=5 define
+
+  I_{2k+1}=G_{k+1}G_{k-3}+1      if k is even,
+  I_{2k+1}=G_kG_{k-2}+1          if k is odd.
+
+For k>=7 define
+
+  I_{2k}=G_{k+2}G_{k-5}+G_3+1    if k is even,
+  I_{2k}=G_{k+1}G_{k-4}+G_3+1    if k is odd,
+
+  J_{2k}=G_{k+1}G_{k-3}+1        if k is even,
+  J_{2k}=G_kG_{k-2}+1            if k is odd.
+
+For m=2k>=10, the maximizing residues are
+
+  {I_{2k+1}-1, G_{2k}-1-I_{2k+1}}.
+
+For m=2k-1>=13, they are
+
+  {I_{2k}-1,J_{2k}-1,G_{2k-1}-1-I_{2k},G_{2k-1}-1-J_{2k}}.
+
+The remaining sets are
+
+  M_0={0}; M_1={0,1}; M_2={0}; M_3={0,1,3}; M_4={0,3};
+  M_5={3}; M_6={3,8}; M_7={3,8,11,16}; M_8={8,16,24};
+  M_9={16,24,29,37}; M_11={37,63,71,79,105};
+
+the generic even formula covers m=10 and m=12. The maximum therefore has exactly two
+maximizers in generic even windows and four in generic odd windows.
+
+4. Local fiber spectrum and frozen unnormalized limits. Let Psi(k) be Weinstein's number
+of generating orbits at multiplicity k and let
+
+  C_m(k)=|{x in X_m:d_m(x)=k}|.
+
+For every m>=1, C_m(1)=2, at residues F_{m+1}-1 and F_{m+2}-1. For every k>=2,
+
+  C_m(k)<=4Psi(k),
+  C_m(k)=4Psi(k) when m>=2k.
+
+Thus the discrete measures sum_k C_m(k)delta_k converge vaguely to
+
+  2delta_1+4 sum_{k>=2}Psi(k)delta_k.
+
+For s>sigma_0,
+
+  S_{-s}(m) -> Zcal(s):=4/{2-zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)}-2.
+
+More exactly, S_t(m) has this finite limit for t< -sigma_0 and diverges to
+infinity for t>=-sigma_0. As s decreases to sigma_0,
+
+  Zcal(s)=4/{kappa(s-sigma_0)}+O(1),
+
+where
+
+  kappa=-[d/ds zeta(s-1)/zeta(s)]_{s=sigma_0}.
+
+For fixed s>sigma_0, the multiplicity Gibbs laws
+C_m(k)k^{-s}/S_{-s}(m) converge in total variation to
+
+  Q_s(1)=2/Zcal(s),
+  Q_s(k)=4Psi(k)k^{-s}/Zcal(s), k>=2.
+
+If K has law Q_s, then, as s decreases to sigma_0,
+
+  E_s log K=1/(s-sigma_0)+O(1),
+  Var_s(log K)=1/(s-sigma_0)^2+O(1).
+
+At the endpoint itself,
+
+  S_{-sigma_0}(m) ~ 4m/mu_C.
+
+If Xhat_m has the finite-window critical Gibbs law proportional to
+d_m(x)^{-sigma_0}, and ell_m(x) is the cost of the generating orbit for d_m(x)>2
+(zero otherwise), then
+
+  (m^{-1}log d_m(Xhat_m),m^{-1}ell_m(Xhat_m))
+       -> (v_c U,U) in distribution.
+
+5. Orbit growth, pressure recovery, and finite-window large deviations. The generator
+counts obey the Tauberian laws
+
+  sum_{k<=x}Psi(k) ~ x^{sigma_0}/(sigma_0 kappa),
+  sum_{k<=x}Psi(k)k^{-sigma_0} ~ (1/kappa)log x.
+
+The paper derives the exact weighted renewal identities
+
+  Z_j^R(-s)=2 sum_{ell=0}^{j-1}u_ell(s)+u_j(s)-1,
+  S_{-s}(m)=4 sum_{j=0}^{m-1}u_j(s)+3u_m(s)+u_{m+1}(s)-2,
+
+where u_0(s)=1, u_j(s)=sum_{ell=1}^j b_ell(s)u_{j-ell}(s), and
+
+  b_ell(s)=sum_{p/q reduced in (0,1), c(p/q)=ell}q^{-s}.
+
+These formulas give the critical constants and the marked coexistence laws. The pressure
+statement above is also recovered in these renewal coordinates. If X_m is uniform on X_m
+and Y_m=m^{-1}log d_m(X_m), then Y_m satisfies the same full LDP and rate I as the
+one-layer R model, with effective domain [0,alpha_+] and the exact local exponential rate
+
+  lim_{epsilon->0} lim_{m->infinity}m^{-1}log
+      Pr{|Y_m-alpha|<epsilon}
+    =-[log phi-sigma_0 alpha]
+
+for every 0<alpha<v_c. The new step in that lower bound is a parity-complete orbit-filling
+injection which embeds source generators into every sufficiently large target layer without
+changing multiplicity.
+
+6. Exact finite-prime-support interface, with no claimed directional asymptotic. Fix
+distinct primes P={p_1,...,p_d}. For a nonzero vector a in N^d put
+q_a=product_i p_i^{a_i} and
+
+  A_a(u)=sum_{1<=h<q_a,(h,q_a)=1}u^{c(h/q_a)},
+  A_P(z,u)=sum_{a!=0}A_a(u)z^a.
+
+If U_j(q_n) counts Weinstein generators with R(g)=q_n and L(g)=j, with
+U_0(1)=1, then exactly
+
+  sum_{n in N^d}sum_{j>=0}U_j(q_n)z^n u^j=1/{1-A_P(z,u)},
+
+and, for q_n>2,
+
+  C_m(q_n)=sum_{j>=0}K_m(j)U_j(q_n),
+
+where K_m(j)=4 for j<=m-1, 3 for j=m, 1 for j=m+1, and 0 for j>=m+2.
+At u=1 the series is the rational function
+
+  1/{2-product_{i=1}^d(1-z_i)/(1-p_i z_i)}.
+
+The paper deliberately does not claim a directional coefficient asymptotic or a quenched
+velocity theorem. Already on the dyadic ray the cost has finite first moment but infinite
+second moment and no positive exponential moment. A sharp active-cutoff asymptotic would
+require a lattice stable or semistable local-renewal theorem uniform under multivariate
+exponent conditioning and compatible with the boundary weights 4,3,1; that ingredient is
+not in the manuscript.
+
+Imported results versus claims that are ours
+
+The following are imported and are not novelty claims.
+
+* Sidorov-Vershik's level-m Fibonacci representation frequency is exactly the same finite
+  subset-sum coefficient used here after their convention F_j^(SV)=F_{j+1}; its normalized
+  mass 2^{-m}f_m(k) approximates the Erdos measure.
+* Hu studied local dimensions of the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution. Lau-Ngai gave its
+  exact L^q-spectrum for all q>0. Feng-Olivier proved that the Erdos measure is weak Gibbs,
+  gave its complete spectrum with a negative linear phase, and proved a first-order phase
+  transition. Feng gave the all-real spectrum and the same positive-root/frozen-branch
+  structure. Under the manuscript's convention the exact dictionary is
+
+      P(t)=t log 2-tau_mu(t)log phi.
+
+  Therefore existence of all-real pressure, the positive implicit branch, the frozen
+  negative phase, nondifferentiability at the critical tilt, and the generic Legendre
+  multifractal formalism are prior results.
+* Kocabova-Masakova-Pelantova's classification of the extrema of R and of all maximizing
+  integers is imported. The paper does not claim a new extremal classification.
+* Weinstein's free generator monoid, multiplier multiplicativity, orbit decomposition,
+  eventual layer stabilization, largest-index formulas, and generator Dirichlet series are
+  imported. The Tauberian theorem used on that series is classical.
+* The Stern-Brocot/Knauf denominator pressure, the arithmetic renewal theorem,
+  Wiener-Ikehara, Gartner-Ellis exposed-slope theory, and smooth-point multivariate
+  coefficient methods are imported tools. Sanna's positive-integer power-sum asymptotics
+  and integer zero-temperature endpoint are prior work. Exact single-value formulas and
+  related results of Chow-Slattery, Chow-Jones, Shallit, Kempton, and others are treated as
+  prior context rather than absorbed into our claims.
+
+What we claim as the surviving contribution is narrower: the pointwise affine fold/subset-
+sum/partition-difference correspondence and two-layer interval identity; exact transfer of
+all maximizing residues; the uniform early-layer bound; the cost-weighted renewal in the
+coordinates needed here; the totient/zeta evaluation of Feng's implicit critical condition;
+the constants 2/mu_C and 4/mu_C; the uniform critical coexistence laws; full uniform-layer
+LDP lower bounds at nonexposed slopes, including the joint cost-reward affine face; the
+parity-free orbit filling; the Tauberian constant for Psi; and the exact prime-support
+coefficient interface without an asymptotic claim.
+
+Priority position, stated against our interest
+
+The current audit accepts that the central thermodynamic framework is not ours. In
+particular, the pressure and freezing narrative originally appeared much more novel than it
+is. Sidorov-Vershik, Hu, Lau-Ngai, Feng-Olivier, and Feng are not merely adjacent references;
+they study the same normalized finite representation frequencies and their L^q-spectrum.
+The current manuscript now says this explicitly and includes the affine pressure dictionary.
+Absence of the exact constants or finite-window wording in those papers is not by itself a
+priority proof for us.
+
+The audit also found three live named nearby problems, none solved by the present paper:
+
+* Weinstein (2016), p. 18: describe the set of F-primitive numbers. Our renewal enumerates
+  generators by multiplier and cost but does not prove the order-sensitive comparison
+  needed to identify the least representative at a fixed multiplier.
+* Weinstein (2016), Conjecture 9.1: a convex-hull description of the points (c,F(c)) in the
+  specified layer sets. Our interval identity and extremal theorem determine the top
+  horizontal support and all its maximizers, but not every supporting slope.
+* Weinstein (2022), Conjecture 3.3: the three counts of Fibonacci representations with a
+  prescribed number of parts modulo 3 differ pairwise by at most one, with one pair equal.
+  Our unmarked coefficient/Fourier formulas do not supply the required uniform
+  roots-of-unity cancellation in the part-count variable.
+
+A former possible target, Chow-Slattery Conjecture 1.4, is not open: Zhou's 2023 work states
+that it solves that conjecture and the first three problems on the cited page. It must not be
+offered as a tier-raising target. The three live problems above may also be structurally too
+far from our machinery; please do not select one merely because it is named.
+
+Machine-verification evidence and its limits
+
+The archived reproducibility suite reports 20 passing pytest tests and checksum-pins 15
+scripts, outputs, and data files. Direct exact enumeration checks every finite window
+m=0,...,28 (the largest coefficient array has 832040 entries) for the interval identity,
+closed forms for D_m, the recurrence D_m=D_{m-2}+D_{m-4}, and the complete maximizer
+classification, with zero counterexamples. The real-tilt battery uses windows
+m in {12,16,20,24,28,32} and t from -8 to 10 in steps of 0.25; it checks convexity and
+Legendre-rate shape, frozen-phase behavior, critical constants, weighted renewal identities,
+one-layer and joint identities, local spectra, and the all-real root/layer normalization.
+The archived counts include 3396 local-spectrum checks; 9011 all-real layer checks plus 7938
+bridge checks; 22 symbolic and 210 real-tilt weighted-renewal checks; 44 one-layer checks;
+22 joint one-layer checks; 225 orbit-capacity and 1815 padding checks; and 590 low-reward
+filler checks, all with zero reported numerical failures. A separate exact-rational and
+interval-enclosure calculation proves the strict numerical separation recorded in the
+speed audit and reports PASS.
+
+These computations are sanity checks and counterexample searches only. They are never a
+substitute for proof, do not establish an asymptotic theorem, do not establish priority, and
+do not cure a missing uniformity argument. The manuscript does not claim that this numerical
+battery or a proof assistant has formally proved its theorem chain.
+
+Your previous verdict and what has changed since it
+
+In the previous round you recommended: "Reject; do not recommend handling this as major
+revision and re-review within TAMS." You said that you found no immediate counterexample to
+the core finite identities and regarded the renewal, critical-constant, and nonexposed-slope
+ideas as broadly coherent. But your negative grounds were decisive:
+
+* The priority account was materially wrong because it omitted the direct Erdos-measure and
+  golden-ratio Bernoulli-convolution line. In particular, Lau-Ngai's positive-q formula and
+  Feng/Feng-Olivier's all-real extension and negative first-order transition predated the
+  manuscript, so all-real pressure and freezing could not stand as our headline novelty.
+* You found two load-bearing proof presentations below a TAMS-verifiable standard: the
+  Stern-Brocot/Knauf normalization in the then Lemma 5.7, including possible shift and factor
+  errors, and the parameter-uniform multivariate smooth-point/tail-truncation argument in the
+  then Theorem 5.12.
+* You also requested a fixed Weinstein convention and endpoint lemma, corrected quantifiers
+  and boundary passages in the scalar and joint LDPs, and narrower wording for an operator
+  obstruction. You regarded these as repairable rigor issues, not evidence that the central
+  conclusions were false.
+* Most unfavorably, you said that even after all technical repairs and honest attribution,
+  the remaining results were specialized exact critical refinements, finite-window transfers,
+  and an LDP completion inside an already developed Bernoulli-convolution pressure system,
+  rather than the broad structural advance or deep single core normally needed for TAMS.
+  You expected rejection even after repair and identified Journal of Number Theory as the
+  appropriate level after substantial rewriting.
+
+The present version has responded to, but cannot simply declare victory over, that verdict.
+It now makes Sidorov-Vershik/Hu/Lau-Ngai/Feng-Olivier/Feng central, prints the exact pressure
+dictionary, and labels all-real pressure and freezing as recovery. It adds a term-by-term
+negative-continued-fraction/Knauf bijection, fixes depth and inverse-temperature conventions,
+and prints the first four denominator layers. It no longer asserts the former directional
+prime-support coefficient asymptotic; it retains only the exact generating function and
+states the heavy-tail obstruction. It also spells out the orbit endpoint conventions,
+repairs the LDP parameter order and relative-boundary passage, and makes the one-layer R
+theorem primary. These changes address the priority and proof-form defects you named, but
+they do not by themselves answer your deeper objection that the mathematical increment is
+too specialized to raise the paper's tier. That deeper objection is exactly what we now ask
+you to interrogate constructively.
+
+Questions, in the required order
+
+1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up a tier. State it
+as a theorem with explicit hypotheses and conclusion, not as a topic, program, or vague
+generalization. The result must add independent mathematical content after the priority
+subtractions above. If there is more than one defensible candidate, rank them, but identify
+one first choice. Explain why that exact statement would change a higher-tier referee's
+assessment rather than merely lengthen the manuscript.
+
+2. For each candidate, is it reachable from the machinery already in this paper? Identify
+the precise existing lemmas, constructions, estimates, or dictionaries that would feed its
+proof: for example, the pointwise interval/affine identities, Weinstein free-word and
+weighted-cost renewal, the critical letter law, the orbit-filling injection, the marked
+renewal measure, the local stabilization bound, or the prime-support rational interface.
+Then identify the genuinely new missing ingredient. If the candidate is not reachable from
+what we have, say so plainly and specify what theorem or technique would be needed instead.
+Do not blur "the notation can express it" into "the machinery can prove it."
+
+3. Rank the candidates by success probability times tier impact. Give success probability
+as a number from 0% to 100%, not an adjective. Also score tier impact from 0 to 10 and give
+the product, so the ranking is auditable. Estimate probability for this team starting from
+the present manuscript, not for an ideal specialist with unlimited time. Distinguish a
+proof-risk estimate from a priority-risk estimate when they differ.
+
+4. Decide which of the four standard tier-raising levers applies here, and state exactly
+how:
+
+   (a) settling a named open problem in the literature;
+   (b) proving something about the field's own standard objects, especially R, the Erdos
+       measure, or the golden-ratio Bernoulli convolution, rather than about Fold_m;
+   (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one the paper currently
+       assumes for convenience;
+   (d) proving a sharpness or matching-bound theorem showing that an existing estimate
+       cannot be improved.
+
+For any applicable lever, give the exact target statement and the exact prior theorem,
+conjecture, hypothesis, or unmatched bound to which it responds. If none applies in a
+reachable way, say that. In particular, do not call the exact transfer of a known statement
+to Fold_m an instance of (b), and do not treat a closed conjecture as (a).
+
+5. Assume the best candidate is proved in full and the current rigor/priority repairs are
+accepted. What is then the strongest objection a referee at the higher tier would still
+raise? State the objection in referee language and state exactly what would resolve it. If
+nothing realistically resolvable would answer it, identify the structural ceiling.
+
+Hard constraints on your answer
+
+Do not answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know where
+the paper lands as it stands. We are asking what mathematical result would move it up.
+
+Do not propose merely adding more theorems of the kind already present. In an earlier round
+we mechanically accumulated formally new consequences and moved nothing; six of our seven
+papers were then found to have priority omissions, because statements composed from standard
+equivalences can be formally new while carrying little independent priority. We need one
+result with a recognizable external mathematical target, not another layer of transfer,
+repackaging, finite-size constants, or thermodynamic terminology.
+
+If the honest answer is that no reachable result exists and this paper cannot rise, say so
+plainly and explain what structural feature caps it. A clear, well-argued ceiling is useful
+and will be acted on; vague encouragement is not. A hard, specific negative is more useful
+than a soft positive, and every objection must come with what would resolve it.
